No one has shown me a single piece of evidence that proves otherwise, so I know they're listening to my calls.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to keep your thoughts organized or to follow a logical sequence when talking or thinking about something?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I do. They felt I couldn't even navigate public events safely on my own. But then... if someone questions my plan, or if my cousin tells me I need to slow down, I just get furious. It's like they're trying to erase me and the work I'm doing. description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: I can sleep two or three hours, wake before dawn feeling completely ready, and tell myself It happens mostly in my apartment, and sometimes when I'm in empty museum rooms. It sounds exactly like women whispering from behind the walls, and they keep telling me which photographs belong in the archive. It's just been happening a lot, so I figured I needed to come in and talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices tell you to do anything or influence your actions in any way?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. They don't give me orders or... or make me do things, if that's what you mean. It's just what I said, them whispering about the photographs and the archive. I mean, my mood has been fine, really. I haven't been feeling sad or down at all... it's just those voices that are the issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices you hear are real?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, I hear them clearly, so they are real to me. And... well, I am convinced the museum board and the city arts people have been monitoring my phone anyway. They know my Caribbean archive will change how the galleries tell the truth. Nobody has shown me anything that proves otherwise, so... yes, I believe it's all very real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the museum board and the city arts people are actually against you or trying to harm you in any way?\n\nPatient: I don't know if they want to physically hurt me, but... yes, they are definitely against me. Like I just mentioned, I am absolutely convinced the museum board and the city arts people are monitoring my phone. They're doing it because they know what I have. My Caribbean archive is going to completely change how the galleries tell the truth, and they want to keep an eye on me because of that. It's just a fact. No one has shown me a single piece of evidence that proves otherwise, so I know they're listening to my calls.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to keep your thoughts organized or to follow a logical sequence when talking or thinking about something?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I do. For example, when my cousin asks me a simple question about a bill or an appointment, I try to answer her. But then I start explaining the grant... and then the shipping routes... and then my grandmother’s altar. By the time I stop talking, nobody seems to know what I meant. So, yes, I guess it can be hard to just follow a straight line when I'm explaining things.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty completing tasks or achieving your goals due to your thoughts or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Honestly... I've lost the trust of my colleagues over all this. I've missed board meetings, and I just haven't been paying my bills. It got to the point where my cousin and one of the museum administrators actually had to step in. I just... I couldn't manage my appointments or handle my money anymore. They felt I couldn't even navigate public events safely on my own. So, yes, getting things done has become very difficult.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods where you felt extremely energetic, happy, or irritable, and didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. There are days when I just feel... radiant. Like I've been chosen for this specific purpose, and I have all the energy in the world to get it done. I don't really need to sleep when I feel like that. But then... if someone questions my plan, or if my cousin tells me I need to slow down, I just get furious. It's like they're trying to erase me and the work I'm doing. So, yes, I get very energized, but also very irritable if people get in my way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that the museum board and city arts people might not be monitoring your phone?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I am absolutely convinced that they are monitoring my phone. The museum board and the city arts people... they know that my Caribbean archive is going to completely change how the galleries tell the truth. So, no, I haven't really considered that they aren't.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your Caribbean archive will have a significantly greater impact on the world than what most people would expect from such a project?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think I have a unique role in the world, just... just the work I'm doing right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other people or organizations, besides the museum board and city arts people, that are against you or trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so.
